@docs/plans/2026-02-18-screenshot-pipeline-and-homepage.md @src/view/mod.rs @src/bin/profile_render.rs @Cargo.toml @website/v4/src/styles/tokens.css

Other relevant files: src/view/frame.rs, src/theme.rs, website/v4/src/pages/index.astro, Makefile

- I wrote an implementation plan at docs/plans/2026-02-18-screenshot-pipeline-and-homepage.md with 9 tasks across 2 phases. This plan should be read and executed using subagent-driven development (fresh subagent per task, two-stage review after each).

- Phase 1 (Tasks 1-6): Build a headless screenshot binary that renders Token editor to PNG using the real Renderer code path, with YAML scenario files and a Makefile target.
- Phase 2 (Tasks 7-9): Improve the homepage at website/v4/src/pages/index.astro with real screenshots replacing the HTML fake editor, micro-interaction polish, and trust badges.

- The key architectural insight: Renderer in src/view/mod.rs has static methods (render_editor_area, render_sidebar, render_status_bar, render_editor_group, render_splitters) that take Frame + TextPainter as arguments — they don't use self/&self. They just need to be made pub so a headless binary can call them without a window/surface. Task 1 handles this.

- src/bin/profile_render.rs is the reference implementation for headless rendering. Its create_model() function (line ~204) shows exactly how to build an AppModel without a window: create Document, EditorState, EditorArea, wire up splits via update(Msg::Layout(LayoutMsg::SplitFocused(...))). The screenshot binary should follow this same pattern but load real files and apply scenario-specific cursors/scroll/theme.

- The image crate is already in Cargo.toml (line 51) with png feature enabled. The buffer format is ARGB (0xAARRGGBB) and needs converting to RGBA for PNG output.

- Font is embedded via include_bytes!("../../assets/JetBrainsMono.ttf") — no OS dependency, fully deterministic.

- Themes are loaded via token::theme::load_theme(id) for builtin themes (fleet-dark, dark, github-dark, github-light in themes/ directory) or token::theme::from_file(path) for file paths. Theme::from_builtin(id) also works.

- AppModel construction requires these fields: editor_area, ui (UiState::new()), theme, config (EditorConfig::default()), window_size, line_height, char_width, metrics (ScaledMetrics::default()), workspace (None), dock_layout (DockLayout::default()), and debug_overlay (None, cfg(debug_assertions) only).

- Sample files for scenarios exist at samples/syntax/ (sample.rs, sample.ts, sample.py, sample.yaml, etc.) and samples/large_data.csv.

- The website is an Astro site at website/v4/. It uses a Fleet Dark-inspired design system with colors in website/v4/src/styles/tokens.css. The homepage has sections: hero (2-column grid with fake HTML editor), stats, features, languages marquee, showcase (single screenshot.png), download, keys preview, AI section.

- I previously redesigned the keybindings page to a CSV-style table view at website/v4/src/pages/keybindings.astro. The old version is preserved at website/v4/src/pages/keybindings.old.astro.

- Build commands: `make build` for the Rust editor, `cd website/v4 && npx astro build` for the website.

- The Renderer struct (src/view/mod.rs line 66) holds: font, surface, back_buffer, width, height, font_size, line_metrics, glyph_cache, char_width, scale_factor. The static render methods only need Frame, TextPainter, and AppModel — not the Renderer instance.

- Frame (src/view/frame.rs) wraps &mut [u32] with width/height and provides safe drawing primitives. TextPainter (same file, line ~300+) wraps font + glyph_cache and provides draw(), draw_with_highlights(), measure_width().

- Scenario YAML files go in screenshots/scenarios/, PNG output goes to screenshots/output/ by default, and the make target outputs to website/v4/public/.

  "content": "1: # Implementer Subagent Prompt Template\n2: \n3: Use this template when dispatching an implementer subagent.\n4: \n5: ```\n6: Task tool (general-purpose):\n7:   description: \"Implement Task N: [task name]\"\n8:   prompt: |\n9:     You are implementing Task N: [task name]\n10: \n11:     ## Task Description\n12: \n13:     [FULL TEXT of task from plan - paste it here, don't make subagent read file]\n14: \n15:     ## Context\n16: \n17:     [Scene-setting: where this fits, dependencies, architectural context]\n18: \n19:     ## Before You Begin\n20: \n21:     If you have questions about:\n22:     - The requirements or acceptance criteria\n23:     - The approach or implementation strategy\n24:     - Dependencies or assumptions\n25:     - Anything unclear in the task description\n26: \n27:     **Ask them now.** Raise any concerns before starting work.\n28: \n29:     ## Your Job\n30: \n31:     Once you're clear on requirements:\n32:     1. Implement exactly what the task specifies\n33:     2. Write tests (following TDD if task says to)\n34:     3. Verify implementation works\n35:     4. Commit your work\n36:     5. Self-review (see below)\n37:     6. Report back\n38: \n39:     Work from: [directory]\n40: \n41:     **While you work:** If you encounter something unexpected or unclear, **ask questions**.\n42:     It's always OK to pause and clarify. Don't guess or make assumptions.\n43: \n44:     ## Before Reporting Back: Self-Review\n45: \n46:     Review your work with fresh eyes. Ask yourself:\n47: \n48:     **Completeness:**\n49:     - Did I fully implement everything in the spec?\n50:     - Did I miss any requirements?\n51:     - Are there edge cases I didn't handle?\n52: \n53:     **Quality:**\n54:     - Is this my best work?\n55:     - Are names clear and accurate (match what things do, not how they work)?\n56:     - Is the code clean and maintainable?\n57: \n58:     **Discipline:**\n59:     - Did I avoid overbuilding (YAGNI)?\n60:     - Did I only build what was requested?\n61:     - Did I follow existing patterns in the codebase?\n62: \n63:     **Testing:**\n64:     - Do tests actually verify behavior (not just mock behavior)?\n65:     - Did I follow TDD if required?\n66:     - Are tests comprehensive?\n67: \n68:     If you find issues during self-review, fix them now before reporting.\n69: \n70:     ## Report Format\n71: \n72:     When done, report:\n73:     - What you implemented\n74:     - What you tested and test results\n75:     - Files changed\n76:     - Self-review findings (if any)\n77:     - Any issues or concerns\n78: ```",

Their report may be incomplete,\n24:     inaccurate, or optimistic. You MUST verify everything independently.\n25: \n26:     **DO NOT:**\n27:     - Take their word for what they implemented\n28:     - Trust their claims about completeness\n29:     - Accept their interpretation of requirements\n30: \n31:     **DO:**\n32:     - Read the actual code they wrote\n33:     - Compare actual implementation to requirements line by line\n34:     - Check for missing pieces they claimed to implement\n35:     - Look for extra features they didn't mention\n36: \n37:     ## Your Job\n38: \n39:     Read the implementation code and verify:\n40: \n41:     **Missing requirements:**\n42:     - Did they implement everything that was requested?\n43:     - Are there requirements they skipped or missed?\n44:     - Did they claim something works but didn't actually implement it?\n45: \n46:     **Extra/unneeded work:**\n47:     - Did they build things that weren't requested?\n48:     - Did they over-engineer or add unnecessary features?\n49:     - Did they add \"nice to haves\" that weren't in spec?\n50: \n51:     **Misunderstandings:**\n52:     - Did they interpret requirements differently than intended?\n53:     - Did they solve the wrong problem?\n54:     - Did they implement the right feature but wrong way?\n55: \n56:     **Verify by reading code, not by trusting report.**\n57: \n58:     Report:\n59:     - ✅ Spec compliant (if everything matches after code inspection)\n60:     - ❌ Issues found: [list specifically what's missing or extra, with file:line references]\n61: ```",

Provides a simple, safe API for pixel buffer operations instead of\n4: //! direct buffer indexing scattered throughout rendering code.\n5: \n6: use fontdue::Font;\n7: use token::model::editor_area::Rect;\n8: \n9: use super::GlyphCache;\n10: \n11: /// Blend a foreground color onto a background color using alpha compositing.\n12: ///\n13: /// Both colors are in ARGB format (0xAARRGGBB). The alpha value from the\n14: /// foreground color determines the blend ratio.\n15: ///\n16: /// Returns the blended color with full opacity (alpha = 0xFF).\n17: #[inline]\n18: pub fn blend_colors(bg: u32, fg: u32, alpha: f32) -> u32 {\n19:     let bg_r = ((bg >> 16) & 0xFF) as f32;\n20:     let bg_g = ((bg >> 8) & 0xFF) as f32;\n21:     let bg_b = (bg & 0xFF) as f32;\n22: \n23:     let fg_r = ((fg >> 16) & 0xFF) as f32;\n24:     let fg_g = ((fg >> 8) & 0xFF) as f32;\n25:     let fg_b = (fg & 0xFF) as f32;\n26: \n27:     let final_r = (bg_r * (1.0 - alpha) + fg_r * alpha) as u32;\n28:     let final_g = (bg_g * (1.0 - alpha) + fg_g * alpha) as u32;\n29:     let final_b = (bg_b * (1.0 - alpha) + fg_b * alpha) as u32;\n30: \n31:     0xFF000000 | (final_r << 16) | (final_g << 8) | final_b\n32: }\n33: \n34: /// A frame buffer wrapper providing safe drawing primitives.\n35: ///\n36: /// All coordinates are in pixels. Out-of-bounds operations are safely clipped.\n37: pub struct Frame<'a> {\n38:     buffer: &'a mut [u32],\n39:     width: usize,\n40:     height: usize,\n41: }\n42: \n43: impl<'a> Frame<'a> {\n44:     /// Create a new frame from a mutable pixel buffer\n45:     ///\n46:     /// If the buffer is smaller than width*height, dimensions are adjusted\n47:     /// to match the actual buffer size to prevent out-of-bounds access.\n48:     pub fn new(buffer: &'a mut [u32], width: usize, height: usize) -> Self {\n49:         let expected_size = width * height;\n50:         let actual_size = buffer.len();\n51: \n52:         let (width, height) = if actual_size < expected_size && width > 0 {\n53:             // Buffer is smaller than expected - recalculate height to fit\n54:             let adjusted_height = actual_size / width;\n55:             (width, adjusted_height)\n56:         } else {\n57:             (width, height)\n58:         };\n59: \n60:         Self {\n[... omitted lines 61 to 594 ...]",

## Assistant

This is a critical architectural issue. The `view` module is private to the main binary — it's `mod view;` in `main.rs`, not `pub mod view;` in `lib.rs`. The plan's approach of calling `Renderer::render_editor_area()` from `src/bin/screenshot.rs` won't work because the binary can only access the `token` library crate, not `main.rs`'s private modules.

There are two options:
1. Move `view` to `lib.rs` as `pub mod view` (but it has GUI deps like `softbuffer`, `winit`)
2. Have the screenshot binary do its own rendering like `profile_render.rs` does (using `Frame` + `TextPainter` directly)

Since `Frame` and `TextPainter` are defined in `src/view/frame.rs` (which is part of the `view` module in main), they're also not accessible. But looking at the plan more carefully, it expects to expose these via `token::view::*` imports. So the view module (or at least `frame.rs`) needs to be moved/re-exported from the library.

{\n302:         let mut frame = Frame::new(&mut buffer, width, height);\n303:         let mut painter = TextPainter::new(\n304:             &font_info.font,\n305:             &mut glyph_cache,\n306: Renderer::render_editor_area(&mut frame, &mut painter, model, &splitters);\n313:         Renderer::render_splitters(&mut frame, &splitters, model);\n314:         Renderer::render_status_bar(&mut frame, &mut painter, model, width, height);\n315:     }\n316: \n317:     buffer\n318: }\n319: \n320: // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n321: // PNG output\n322: // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n323: \n324: fn save_png(buffer: &[u32], width: u32, height: u32, path: &std::path::Path) -> Result<()> {\n325:     // Convert ARGB (0xAARRGGBB) to RGBA bytes\n326:     let mut rgba = Vec::with_capacity((width * height * 4) as usize);\n327:     for &pixel in buffer {\n328:         let r = ((pixel >> 16) Ok(())\n350: }\n351: \n352: // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n353: // Scenario loading\n354: // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n355: \n356: fn load_scenario(path: &std::path::Path) -> Result<Scenario> {\n357:     let content = .with_context(|| format!(\"parsing scenario {}\", path.display()))?;\n361:     Ok(scenario)\n362: }\n363: \n364: fn collect_scenarios(args: &Args) -> Result<Vec<(PathBuf, Scenario)>> {\n365:     let mut scenarios = Vec::new();\n366: \n367:     if let Some(ref path) = args.scenario {\n368:         let scenario = load_scenario(path)?;\n369:         scenarios.push((path.clone(), scenario));\n370:   .collect();\n389:         entries.sort_by_key(|e| e.path());\n390: \n391:         for entry in entries {\n392:             let path = entry.path();\n393:             match load_scenario(&path) {\n394:                 Ok(scenario) => scenarios.push((path, scenario)),\n395:                 Err(e) => eprintln!(\"Warning: skipping {}: {}\", path.display(), e),\n396:             }\n397: ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n413: // Main\n414: // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n415: \n416: fn main() -> The screenshot renders correctly with a real editor look! There are two issues:

## Assistant

So the issue is that when we split and replace the document buffer, all splits share the same document. The `SplitFocused` message clones the current document, so when we replace the focused document's buffer, it changes the NEW document (which is in the newly focused group), not the original. But it seems all panes end up showing the last loaded content.

## Assistant

I see — splitting creates a new editor for the **same document** (shared doc_id). When we then call `focused_document_mut()` and replace the buffer, we're modifying the SAME shared document, so ALL editors pointing to it see the change.

The fix: instead of replacing the buffer in the shared document, we need to create a new document for each split. Let me update the screenshot binary:

## Assistant

All 9 tasks implemented across 5 commits:

| Commit | Tasks |
|--------|-------|
| `6d08bcd` | **Task 1**: Made render methods pub + moved view module to lib.rs (with perf module extraction) |
| `c2d2b61` | **Tasks 2-4**: Screenshot binary with CLI args, YAML scenario schema, headless render + PNG output |
| `510fc85` | **Task 5**: 5 scenario YAML files (hero, multi-cursor, splits, csv, minimal) |
| `8de36c5` | **Task 6**: `make screenshots` Makefile target + fixed split document sharing bug |
| `3a71ddb` | **Tasks 7-9**: Real screenshot hero, showcase with splits, window chrome, trust badges, micro-interactions (card hover lift, CTA arrow, screenshot glow) |

Key architectural change: moved the `view` module from `main.rs` to `lib.rs` so binaries in `src/bin/` can call `Renderer::render_editor_area()` etc. directly. This required extracting `PerfStats` to `src/perf.rs` in the library and updating runtime references.
